OUR STORY
Changes are afoot in mid-19th century Derbyshire,
England. The envy of this part of the county is Alderley
Hall, where Margaret, Dowager Countess Fairchild, and
her sister-in-law, Ethel, reside with a joint income well in
excess of £20,000 pa. Only their loneliness living on such                bsession
a sprawling estate dampens the generous hearts of the two
widowed and childless ladies.     OThen, to their delight, they welcome new occupants:       Universal
dashing nephew Charles and lovely niece Elizabeth, sadly
not far removed from tragedy. Their parents, returningfrom holiday in the south of France, perished crossing the       Rules
Channel in sudden ill weather. The deaths of Margaret’s
brother-in-law and his lady have brought Charles andElizabeth permanently to the Fairchild residence, far from  Compendium
their childhood estate in Yorkshire.                                         A Kayenta Games publicationOf course, the irrepressible Fairchild ladies—now a year                                                                        by Dan Hallaganremoved from the terrible accident—know precisely what
the best medicine for a sad heart is: romance! Fortunately,          Rulebook Editing          Advisors
the surrounding hills are awash in prime matchmaking                  Jeff Fraser                 David Buckland
material. Having endured the hard times of the late 18th                             Tim Hallagan                                                    Rulebook Layout
century, the respectable families of Derbyshire are intent                                    Renée Harris                                                    Matt Paquette & Co.
on restoring their country estates and reputations, not to                                                                                            Editorial Review                                                    Appendix Developmentmention finding matches for their marriageable children.                                                                            Matt Sumski                                                               Christian LiljebergA sense of optimism is abroad, and the Fairchild ladies                                                                                                                                        J. Sebastian Pagani
have designs!    CHAPTER 1. INTEGRATED SETUP

    This section lists the steps to set up
    the game with or without expansions.            Designer’s Note:                                                                        In early 2024, I went to one of my many   Follow the basic Player Setup to set up                                                         Kallaxes and gathered up four boxes for one of my favourite    a standard-length game for 2-4 players
    without any expansions. Follow the               games—the base and three expansions. Friends were an hour
    appropriately labelled extra steps to include         away, and, as the resident rules guru, I needed to brush up. After 10
    expansions and/or common variants.                 minutes of flipping through 4 rulebooks, I packed everything up and
                                                      grabbed a different game; I just didn’t have time to go through it all.  TEAMS, CO-OP, AND OTHER
                                                      Later that evening, I realised that I had skipped playing a beloved  VARIANTS
                                               game because getting it to the table had become unwieldy.
   To set up other official variants, see Chapter 4,
                                                     The Obsession family has 9 rules references scattered over 5 titles!    Optional Rules & Content (pg. 46).
                                                            This compendium is designed to put answers to every rule question   To set up other game modes such as Tableau
    Obsession, Team Obsession or Cooperative               at your fingertips, to rescue Obsession from the very un-Victorian
    Obsession, see Chapter 6, Alternate Game                fate of being…cumbersome.
   Modes (pg. 62).

                   SETUP – EXTRA STEPS REFERENCE

                   What is it? Expansions add a variety of gameplay features, including new core(U) Upstairs, Downstairs                          mechanics, playable families, servant types, gentry, and improvement tiles. Except where(C) Characters                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       Components                          otherwise noted, all expansions and optional modules are compatible with each other.(W) Wessex                            Feel free to mix and match to create your perfect experience!(B) Promo Tiles Pack
                   What do I need? The indicated expansion(s).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           SetupSetup
                   What is it? An extended game lasts 20 rounds instead of the standard 16.(X) Extended
                   What do I need? Just the base game. Compatible with all expansions.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     IntegratedIntegrated
                   What is it? In solo mode, a single player faces automated opponents of various difficulties.
(1P) Solo            What do I need? Just the base game for a standard-length game. An extended game requires                                                                                                                                                                                                                     Game                          extended solo opponents from Wessex; Upstairs, Downstairs; or Characters.
                                                                                                                                                                the
                   What is it? Adds up to 2 additional players to the game.
                   What do I need?                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                Playing
                     (U) 5 Players: Requires 5–6 player components from Upstairs, Downstairs and
(5P+) 5–6 Players          at least 1 expansion family from Upstairs, Downstairs or Wessex.
                     (U) (W) 6 Players: Requires 5–6 player components from Upstairs, Downstairs and                                          Detail                        both expansion families from Upstairs, Downstairs and Wessex.
                     Team variant is recommended (see 6.2 Team Mode, pg. 64).                                                                                    Rules

   CHAPTER 2. PLAYING THE GAME
                  2.1 OVERVIEW

                                               In Obsession, each player takes the helm of a 19th-century family striving
                                                    to improve their diminished social standing. Over the course of the game,
                                                          you’ll use your family members and domestic servants to expand your country
                                                           estate, host a variety of influential guests, and strive to secure a match with the
                                                      prestigious Fairchild family. At the end of the game, you score victory points
                                          (VP) for your progress across a variety of categories. The player who scores the
                                           most total VP wins the game.
    OUR STORY
         Six of Derbyshire’s respectable
        families—each possessed of eligible                  2.2 SEASONS & ROUNDS        young gentlemen and ladies—find
         themselves embroiled in romance                                                   Game Rounds        and intrigue during a time of              The game is divided into 4 seasons.
         optimism. Though they have              Each season is made up of 3 game rounds,
         long and storied histories in the               followed by a courtship event.
          county, the difficulties of the late                • During each game round, each player takes
        18th century have left the families            1 turn, starting with the first player and going
         Asquith, Cavendish, Howard,                  clockwise. After the last player’s turn, the
         Ponsonby, Wessex, and York with            round marker advances 1 space.
          neglected country estates and                   •                                  When the marker reaches a courtship event,         tarnished reputations. Now, in the                                                      players determine who claims a Fairchild        mid-19th, they have coincidentally                                                   card for the following season.    The Ponsonby player earns a total of 3 reputation and

   them draw 4 new casual guests, choose 2 to keep, and
   return the others to the bottom of the deck. The Lady
   Ponsonby provides 2 options: either draw 2 casual guests                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         Appendices   and choose 1, or permanently dismiss 1 guest. The player
    decides to dismiss a guest with a negative VP value from
    their discard pile.

    2.6 COURTSHIP & END OF SEASON
   The arrival of Charles and Elizabeth Fairchild—both eligible,  COURTSHIP EVENTS
    refined, and substantially wealthy—into a relatively modest
    corner of Derbyshire engages the minds, hearts, and conversation    Each time the round marker reaches a courtship space, follow
    of every member of the social elite within a day’s ride by carriage.     the steps below to resolve a courtship event.
   Each season ends with a courtship event, where one
   Derbyshire family is given the opportunity for their son or      ORDER OF PLAY
   daughter to court one of the young Fairchilds. The winning      (COURTSHIP EVENT)
    family receives a Fairchild card of their choice for the                                                                         1. Return Fairchilds: Place both Fairchild cards on the    following season, along with a VP card.                                                                       Alderley Hall board, if they are not already there.
                                                                         2. Score Theme: The theme card revealed at the        EXAMPLE: SCORE THEME                                                                                 start of the season determines which category
        This is the third season of the game, so 3 theme cards                 of improvement tiles is used to score this event.
       have been revealed so far. For the current season, only            Each player adds up the total VP value of their
        the Estate improvements are scored:                           improvement tiles that match the theme to get their
                                                           theme score. (Note: Tiles with negative VP values
                                                                           that match the theme reduce your theme score.)
                                                                         3. Winner: The player with the highest theme score is
                                                                       the winner. They draw 1 VP card and take 1 Fairchild
                                                                      card of their choice from Alderley Hall, adding it to
                                                                               their hand.
                                                                         4. Discard Objectives: Each player chooses 1 of their
                                                                            objective cards and discards it face down (pg. 42).
                                                                         Shuffle the discarded objectives and place them on
                                                                      the bottom of the objective deck.


                                    COURTSHIP TIES
                                                                                  If 2 or more players tie for highest theme score during a
                                                                  courtship event, each tied player draws 1 VP card, and
                                                   no one takes a Fairchild card.

                                    COURTSHIP REWARDS
                                           When you draw a VP card, you may either immediately
                                                                        reveal and discard it to gain its printed benefit or keep it face
                                                  down with your objective cards. If you keep it, you may either
                                                                    discard it for its benefit on a later turn or keep it until the end
                                                                    of the game to score its VP value. See VP cards, pg. 42 for
                                                      more detail.
                                                              Charles and Elizabeth Fairchild are prestige guests who may
                                                           be played, discarded, and returned the same as your other
                                                                  guest cards. However, like family members, the Fairchilds
                                                             have no prestige rating and may be played at any reputation
                                                                             level (see Appendix B: Gentry Cards, pg. 80).
                                                    At the start of each courtship, the winner of the previous
                                                                  courtship must return to Alderley Hall the Fairchild card they       The winning player’s Estate improvements are worth
                                                             took (whether they are in the player’s hand or discard pile).       2+(-2)+2+4 = 6 points. They draw a VP card and choose                                                             In order to score the VP on a Fairchild card, you must win       a Fairchild card to add to their hand.
                                                     them during the final courtship event (pg. 23).

  SERVICE ABILITIES
   Some servant types provide additional benefits when used.
   For example, your housekeeper starts the game with the
    ability to substitute for a lady’s maid when no lady’s maids
    are available (see Unique Servants, pg. 29). This means you
   may use her to fulfill a service need for a lady’s maid on any

    rest of the game, and only return to the deck if you dismiss      unsavoury guests*. All casual guests who are not unsavoury
   them (see below).                                                  are considered respectable. Only casual guests can be
                                                               unsavoury or respectable; prestige guests and family are
                                                                   considered separate categories (even if they have red frames).
       Designer’s Note: Obsession also uses the term
           ‘invite’ for playing gentry during an activity
          (pg. 18) and taking characters from the sideboard
          (pg. 43). While a Victorian family would have
         well understood the distinction between a formal                             Examplesunsavouryof
                                                                                     guests         introduction and inviting a friend to tea,
          I acknowledge these repeated terms can cause some
          confusion in gameplay. These rules continue to use
           ‘invite’ in multiple ways to preserve compatibility
        with existing rulebooks and components.
                                                             *The one exception are Exiles (C), who are always considered
                                                                         respectable, even if they are casual and have a red frame.

   The Builders’ Market reflects competition for scarce skilled  THE BUILDERS’ RESERVE
    labour and high-end building materials in rural mid-19th
   century England. When planning the expansion of your       As the game progresses, local builders increase their efforts
    estate, you must work within the funds you have and the         to meet the rising demand for construction, and simpler
    opportunities that are available.                           improvement projects become more affordable. This is

Market each turn, either when hosting an activity or
    passing. The Builders’ Holiday and certain VP cards allow

   purchase tiles, you must refill the Market from the draw          Designer’s Note: On the game board, this area

            CHAPTER 4.
   OPTIONAL RULES & CONTENT

   Chapter 4 contains critical,       Here you will find milestones, a balanced Charles Fairchild card, monument options, and
    routinely-used variations that      the asymmetric Distinguished Family Members module as well as the most popular game
    are fundamentally part of          length: extended play. These are not whimsical variants, but an essential toolbox for arriving
    high-level Obsession contests.       at your preferred style of play. Note: See pg. 43 for the Reserving Characters variant.
                 The Variant Catalogue in the Characters expansion contains additional fan-based variants.
         4.1 (X) EXTENDED GAME

      •                   GAMEPLAY      What is it? Adds 1 more round to each season.
      • Why use it? Allows for more developed country          Each season has 4 game rounds (instead of the standard 3),
         estates, more elaborate activities, dramatically higher       meaning each player will take 16 turns during the game
        reputation scores, and a better chance to complete            (instead of 12). The maximum reputation level is increased
          difficult objectives.                                          to 9, allowing players to score additional Reputation VP at
                                                    game end (see End Game Scoring, pg. 24).
                                                          Extended Play has 3 Village Fairs instead of 2 and locates
                                                                  the National Holiday early in Season 3.
       COMPONENTS & MODULES
                                      OBJECTIVES
    Requires:    Reputation Level 7/8 Counters                                                       Over the course of the game, each player draws a total of
                                                        8 objective cards (4 at setup and 4 more during 2 Draw
                                                                  Objectives round track events). As normal, players must
                                                                     discard 1 objective at the end of each courtship event,
 SETUP                                                  leaving each player with 4 objectives at game end. Players
                                                   may score all 4 of their remaining objectives, if able.

       CHAPTER 5. SOLO PLAY

   The central gameplay mechanic of
    Obsession is the hosting of social                                        Designer’s Note: My advice for every new player is to begin with a solo    activities, which is an action that takes
    place independent of one’s competitors.          game. Not only will the thematically coherent rules fall into place, but you see
   As a result, Obsession naturally lends              the central puzzle of the game in action: the interaction of reputation, guests,
    itself to solo play. Starting with a solo            servants, and improvements, which will greatly help you learn the game.
   game is the very best way for a new
    player to learn the game.
              5.1 SOLO MODE

 ORDER OF PLAY (AI TURN)
                                                                                                                                                                                                                       PlayPlay  1. Check for Monuments: If there is at least
     1 monument in the Builders’ Market, your opponent uses                                                                                                                                                                                                                       SoloSolo
     the ‘Monument Turn’ column on the solo AI card this turn.
     Otherwise, they use the ‘Standard Turn’ column.
  2. Acquire from Market: Roll the d20 solo dice and resolve
     the matching-numbered result on the solo AI card (in the                                                                                                                                           Modes
      appropriate column).
       •  1-15 (Standard Turn): Your opponent takes the indicated
           tile (or tiles) from one space in the Builders’ Market.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    Alternate
       •  1-15 (Monument Turn): Your opponent takes the indicated
           tile (or tiles) from one space in the Builders’ Market. If
         there is more than one monument, the AI targets the least                     Solo AI Card
         expensive one.       •  16-19: Your opponent doesn’t do anything.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     Appendices
       •  20: Your opponent refreshes the Builders’ Market (pg. 41).
  3. Advance Round Marker: Advance the round marker 1 space.

                                                                               5.1 Solo Mode                                                                  55
--- Page 56 ---
  TAKING TILES
                                       EXAMPLE: SOLO COURTSHIP SCORING
  When your opponent rolls a numbered position, they
    take all tiles in the corresponding space on the Builders’
   Market track (including duplicates). Market track spaces are
   numbered 1-6 from left to right (£300 to £800). They never
    take tiles from the Builders’ Reserve.
    All tiles your opponent takes from the Market are
   permanently removed from the game; do not return them
    to the draw bag. After your opponent takes tiles, refill the
   Market normally (pg. 41).

     EXAMPLE: NUMBERED POSITIONS



                                                               At the end of     1 2 3 4 5 6                                                                             season 1, a Prestige

  REMOVING TILES AT END OF TURN
   At the end of each turn, you must randomly select 1 tile          After you roll, advance the round marker 1 space on the
   from the Builders’ Market to discard from the game.           round track and begin your next turn.
   To do this, roll the d20 solo dice and find the solo estate
   counter that includes your result in its number range.                 Critical Rule:                                                                             You cannot remove a tile    If you rolled the lowest number in the counter’s range,                                                                                 that would make it impossible for you to   remove the top tile on the corresponding stack from the                                                                       complete your tableau. Whenever you would   game. If you rolled the second-lowest number, remove the                                                                do so, ignore your roll result and do not   second tile from the top of the stack (and so on). If there is                                                                   remove a tile that turn.   no tile corresponding to the number you rolled, do not remove
   a tile this turn. (This includes when you roll a 19 or 20.)


                         EXAMPLE: REMOVING TILES
                       In a standard-length game, you have 2 Service tiles out of the 3 you need in your tableau.
                       You roll a 1, matching the Service stack. However, there is only 1 tile left. Since removing
                                  it would mean you can’t complete your tableau, you ignore your roll.

   You do not draw any objectives                          When you have fulfilled all 3 conditions to complete your tableau, the game ends at    at setup and don’t discard any                                       the end of your current turn (before advancing the round marker).   during the game.    draw objectives using the Main      Count your VP in each category as you would in a normal multiplayer game (pg. 24).
   Library and VP cards. These       Add a VP bonus or penalty based on the final position of the round marker relative to
    objectives are scored normally.        the highlighted target round space on the track.
                                              •  If you completed your tableau during the target round, you get no bonus or penalty.
                                              •  If you completed your tableau before the target round, you get a bonus.
                                        Score +5 VP for each round you finished early.
                                              •  If you completed your tableau after the target round, you get a penalty. Lose 5 VP for
                                        each round you finished late (i.e., if you finish 4 rounds late you lose 20 VP).
                                Compare your final score to the target VP value printed next to the target round on
                                       the Alderley Hall board (132 VP for a standard-length game, 188 VP for an extended-
                                        length game). If you beat the target value, you win the game.
                               Opponent Challenge Variants: If you’re ready to increase the difficulty of the Solo
                                        Estate Challenge, you can use the base VP value on a solo opponent card instead of
                                      the target VP value on the round track; you must use a solo opponent from base game or
                                        Wessex expansion. Optionally, add scavenged monument VPs to the solo opponent base
                                          score for maximum difficulty.

            CHAPTER 6.
     ALTERNATE GAME MODES

   These 3 game modes—Tableau, Team, and
   Cooperative—are not variants, but complete
   game modes each with a specific emphasis.
   Tableau Mode—aka Obsession Chess—
   removes almost all the thematic randomness
    that represents Victorian fate; Team Mode
    leverages the thematic fun of partnering with
   another player; and the Cooperative Mode
    challenges players to stop the loathsome
   Sneyd Family from stealing away the lovely
    Elizabeth.

              6.2 TEAM MODE
   While gossip was the lifeblood of the Victorian social drama,   GAMEPLAY
   enduring friendships and strategic relationships were not
   uncommon. What could be better than speaking truth about your      Seasons, rounds, and turns are resolved as in a normal
    self-promoting, unscrupulous neighbours? Why, doing it with the      multiplayer game. However, throughout the game, players
    dearest of family friends, of course, over tea and biscuits.             can assist their allies by sharing money, gentry cards, and
                                                                      servants (according to the restrictions detailed below). Allied
                                                                        players may also openly discuss strategy and show each other      • What is it? Players split into 2-player teams that                                                             any secret information, such as objective cards, at any time.
       compete against each other. Allied players coordinate                                                     At the end of the game, each team calculates a single score         objectives and work together by sharing money,                                                               based on the average performance of both players (see End         gentry, and servants. Combines elements of cooperative                                                              each team scores their best objectives between their players.
       and competitive play. Excellent for introducing                                                        The players on the team with the highest score win together.
      new players to the game by pairing them with
        experienced partners.                      RECEIVING ASSISTANCE

     overwhelmed. Where should I start?                                                         A: Neither of these cards are playable gentry cards; they
      A: Getting expansions right away can be challenging,           are included for fun. However, see the Variant Catalogue
       so please set them aside for now. If you play just the            included in the Characters expansion for a community
       base game to start, the experience will be smooth, and           variant that uses the Duchess Lilo card.
         you’ll quickly learn the mechanics.
         • First Game: I recommend a solitaire game for your
          1st play, even if you don’t normally play solo. Solo
          teaches the flow without the bother of instructing
        someone else. Find a solo video on Youtube
           (I recommend jplay, search jplay Obsession) and
         watch. Set up a relaxed solo game (pg. 54) against
         a Beginner opponent. Start hosting activities and
       make your best guess at the rules you’re not sure
          about. Play in the sandbox. After you’ve done this,
         then go back and read the rulebook, and it will all
             fall into place; play one more solo game to lock it in.   COURTSHIP

=== CORRIGENDUM 1.0 ===
--- Page 1 ---
Universal Rules Compendium
© Kayenta Games, October 6, 2026
Corrigendum 1.0


Major Errata
1.2 Central Play Area Setup, Page 13.
There is a missing (1P) solo play instruction in the (C) SIDEBOARD SETUP section. After the a.
setup step, there should be:

                    b.      (1P) Fill Higbee's with 2 SC servants of each type.
                                         __________

2.6 Courtship & End of Season, Page 23.
There is a missing (1P) (C) solo play instruction in the ORDER OF PLAY (END OF SEASON) section.
There should be an upkeep step as follows:

                    2.5.    (1P) (C) Add the top 2 cards from the objective deck
                           to Reverend Grantly’s pile, face up.

Note: This ensures there are enough objectives to choose from when enjoying the reverend’s
favours.
                                         __________

5.3 Solo Estate Challenge Variant, Page 60.
There is an error in the GAME END & SCORING section (bullet points 2 and 3). The correct rules
are:

    •    If you complete your tableau before the target round, you get a bonus. Score 5 VP if you
       ﬁnish 1 round early. Score 15 VP (5 + 10) if you ﬁnish 2 rounds early. The incremental round
      bonus increases by 5 VP for every round you ﬁnish early. For example, if you ﬁnish 4 rounds
        early, your bonus is 50 VP (+5 +10 +15 +20 = 50).
    •    If you complete your tableau after the target round, you get a penalty. Lose 5 VP if you ﬁnish
      1 round late. Lose 15 VP if you ﬁnish 2 rounds late. The incremental round penalty increases
       by 5 VP for every round you ﬁnish late. For example, if you ﬁnish 4 rounds late, you lose 50
     VP (–5 –10 –15 –20= –50).

Minor Errata
3.2 Servants, Page 32.
There is a missing word in the Favourable Impression section (the word is highlighted in red
below):

                  •  Favourable Impression: When hosting an activity, you may
                    place your hall boy on a positive money favour on a gentry
                    card you played to gain an extra £100. (You may not do this
                  on an improvement tile.)

Corrigendum 1.0


Minor Errata, continued
Appendix B: Gentry Cards, Page 85.
An incorrect title is used in the (U) LADY MARY SHELTON entry. It should read (the correct address
is highlighted in red below):

             Note: Cannot be used in the Queen’s Suite. When drawn into the
              Lionheart Suite, Lady Mary refuses to stay. Return her to the bottom
               of the prestige guest deck and redraw.

                                         __________

Appendix C: Improvement Tiles, Page 88.
There is a missing End Game rule in the Business Room entry (missing rule highlighted in red
below):

           End of game: Worth 1 VP. Money on the tile at game end is scored
              normally (it does not have to be removed).
                                         __________

Player Aid Note: Due to the change in default market churn at the start of each new season (see
Designer’s Note on 2.6 Courtship & End of Season, Page 23), the Seasonal Flowchart player aid in
the base game is incomplete as it does not reference the market refresh. Recommended: Use the
ORDER OF PLAY (END OF SEASON), Page 23 going forward.
